  _[ru]_ ← "(Henry Holt Co, 1958) ISBN 0-89275-011-1 ISBN 0-8159-7216-4 Peter Wetzler, Hirohito and War, University of Hawaii press, 1998 ISBN 082481925X"
- John Costello wrote a book titled 'The Pacific War 1941-1945'  
  _[tr]_ ← "Costello, John, The Pacific War 1941-1945(Pasifik Savaşı 1941-1945) (New York: William Morrow, 1982) ISBN 0-688-01620-0 Robert A. Theobald, Final Secret of Pear…"
- The book 'The Pacific War 1941-1945' was published in New York by William Morrow in 1982  
  _[tr]_ ← "Costello, John, The Pacific War 1941-1945(Pasifik Savaşı 1941-1945) (New York: William Morrow, 1982) ISBN 0-688-01620-0 Robert A. Theobald, Final Secret of Pear…"

### Intelligence and Roosevelt's reaction  *(new section)*
- The convoy was crossing the South China Sea  
  _[es]_ ← "El segundo factor, probablemente el más decisivo, fue un informe de la inteligencia militar sobre movimientos de tropas japonesas en el sudeste de Asia —se habí…"
- Military intelligence reported on Japanese troop movements in Southeast Asia  
  _[es]_ ← "El segundo factor, probablemente el más decisivo, fue un informe de la inteligencia militar sobre movimientos de tropas japonesas en el sudeste de Asia —se habí…"
- The convoy carried approximately 50,000 soldiers  
  _[es]_ ← "El segundo factor, probablemente el más decisivo, fue un informe de la inteligencia militar sobre movimientos de tropas japonesas en el sudeste de Asia —se habí…"
- The report indicated that Japan was preparing to launch an attack at any moment  
  _[es]_ ← "El segundo factor, probablemente el más decisivo, fue un informe de la inteligencia militar sobre movimientos de tropas japonesas en el sudeste de Asia —se habí…"
- The telegram was sent by Minister Togo  
  _[es]_ ← "Esta conclusión venía avalada por un telegrama enviado por el ministro Togo a la embajada de Washington, que había sido descifrado por los servicios secretos no…"
- The telegram was addressed to the embassy in Washington  
  _[es]_ ← "Esta conclusión venía avalada por un telegrama enviado por el ministro Togo a la embajada de Washington, que había sido descifrado por los servicios secretos no…"
- The telegram was deciphered by American intelligence services  
  _[es]_ ← "Esta conclusión venía avalada por un telegrama enviado por el ministro Togo a la embajada de Washington, que había sido descifrado por los servicios secretos no…"
- President Roosevelt became furious upon receiving this information  
  _[es]_ ← "Cuando fue informado de los movimientos de tropas el presidente Roosevelt se puso furioso porque era una prueba de la mala fe de los japoneses y dijo que esto «…"
- Roosevelt considered the troop movements proof of Japanese bad faith  
  _[es]_ ← "Cuando fue informado de los movimientos de tropas el presidente Roosevelt se puso furioso porque era una prueba de la mala fe de los japoneses y dijo que esto «…"
- Roosevelt stated that this situation completely changed  
  _[es]_ ← "Cuando fue informado de los movimientos de tropas el presidente Roosevelt se puso furioso porque era una prueba de la mala fe de los japoneses y dijo que esto «…"
- Roosevelt later believed Japan would attack the United States on December 1  
  _[es]_ ← "Cuando fue informado de los movimientos de tropas el presidente Roosevelt se puso furioso porque era una prueba de la mala fe de los japoneses y dijo que esto «…"
- Roosevelt justified his belief by stating that it is well known that Japan attacks without warning  
  _[es]_ ← "Cuando fue informado de los movimientos de tropas el presidente Roosevelt se puso furioso porque era una prueba de la mala fe de los japoneses y dijo que esto «…"

### Soviet espionage and Operation Snow  *(new section)*
- The Venona Project was one of the most sensitive counter-espionage programs in US history  
  _[fr]_ ← "Il tenait cette information des fichiers du Projet Venona, l'un des plus sensibles et plus secrets programmes de contre-espionnage de l'histoire des États-Unis,…"
- The Venona Project was one of the most secret counter-espionage programs in US history  
  _[fr]_ ← "Il tenait cette information des fichiers du Projet Venona, l'un des plus sensibles et plus secrets programmes de contre-espionnage de l'histoire des États-Unis,…"
- The Venona Project was declassified only in 1995  
  _[fr]_ ← "Il tenait cette information des fichiers du Projet Venona, l'un des plus sensibles et plus secrets programmes de contre-espionnage de l'histoire des États-Unis,…"
- According to the project's information, many Soviet spies played an important role  
  _[fr]_ ← "D'après les informations du projet, de nombreux espions soviétiques ont joué un rôle important pour contraindre l'Empire du Japon à entrer en guerre contre les …"
- These spies forced the Japanese Empire to enter a war against the United States  
  _[fr]_ ← "D'après les informations du projet, de nombreux espions soviétiques ont joué un rôle important pour contraindre l'Empire du Japon à entrer en guerre contre les …"
- The Venona documents were published in later years  
  _[ja]_ ← "しかし、後年に公開されたヴェノナ文書によると、当時のソ連の諜報活動はアメリカ政府中枢にも及んでおり、その中でもホワイトは最も高い地位にいた人物であったと思われる。"
- According to the Venona documents, Soviet intelligence activities at that time reached the center of the US government  
  _[ja]_ ← "しかし、後年に公開されたヴェノナ文書によると、当時のソ連の諜報活動はアメリカ政府中枢にも及んでおり、その中でもホワイトは最も高い地位にいた人物であったと思われる。"
- Harry White was considered to be one of the highest-ranking individuals involved in these activities  
  _[ja]_ ← "しかし、後年に公開されたヴェノナ文書によると、当時のソ連の諜報活動はアメリカ政府中枢にも及んでおり、その中でもホワイトは最も高い地位にいた人物であったと思われる。"
- White was not acting under orders from the American Communist Party  
  _[ja]_ ← "ただし、ホワイトはアメリカ共産党から命令を受けていたわけではなく、あくまで自主的な活動・協力にとどまっており、情報源としての評価も高いものではなかった。"
- White's activities were limited to independent actions and cooperation  
  _[ja]_ ← "ただし、ホワイトはアメリカ共産党から命令を受けていたわけではなく、あくまで自主的な活動・協力にとどまっており、情報源としての評価も高いものではなかった。"
- White was not considered a high-value intelligence source  
  _[ja]_ ← "ただし、ホワイトはアメリカ共産党から命令を受けていたわけではなく、あくまで自主的な活動・協力にとどまっており、情報源としての評価も高いものではなかった。"
- Pavlov made contact with White  
  _[ja]_ ← "後のKGB)で米国部副部長を務めたヴィターリー・パヴロフがホワイトと接触し、アメリカの交渉戦略に関する情報等を提供していたことが、1990年代になって明らかとなった。"
- Pavlov provided information regarding American negotiation strategies to White  
  _[ja]_ ← "後のKGB)で米国部副部長を務めたヴィターリー・パヴロフがホワイトと接触し、アメリカの交渉戦略に関する情報等を提供していたことが、1990年代になって明らかとなった。"
- It was revealed in the 1990s that Pavlov had contacted White and provided such information  
  _[ja]_ ← "後のKGB)で米国部副部長を務めたヴィターリー・パヴロフがホワイトと接触し、アメリカの交渉戦略に関する情報等を提供していたことが、1990年代になって明らかとなった。"
- The Soviet side referred to White by the name 'Operation Snow'  
  _[ja]_ ← "ソ連側ではホワイトの名前から「雪作戦」と呼ばれていた。"
- Pavlov stated the purpose of Operation Snow  
  _[ja]_ ← "パヴロフは「雪作戦」の目的を関東軍の脅威の中で、ソ連極東地域を日本の攻撃と侵攻から防衛することだったとしている。"
- The purpose was to defend the Soviet Far East region from Japanese attacks and invasions  
  _[ja]_ ← "パヴロフは「雪作戦」の目的を関東軍の脅威の中で、ソ連極東地域を日本の攻撃と侵攻から防衛することだったとしている。"
- This defense was necessary amidst the threat posed by the Kwantung Army  
  _[ja]_ ← "パヴロフは「雪作戦」の目的を関東軍の脅威の中で、ソ連極東地域を日本の攻撃と侵攻から防衛することだったとしている。"
- In return, the United States would provide economic compensation to Japan  
  _[ja]_ ← "具体的にはアメリカの圧力で満洲の関東軍を撤退させ、その見返りにアメリカが日本に経済的埋め合わせを行うというもので、ホワイトが書いた私案にもそれらが踏襲された(パヴロフが直接ホワイトと会ったのは、1941年5月の一度だけとのことである)。"
- The plan involved withdrawing the Kwantung Army from Manchuria under American pressure  
  _[ja]_ ← "具体的にはアメリカの圧力で満洲の関東軍を撤退させ、その見返りにアメリカが日本に経済的埋め合わせを行うというもので、ホワイトが書いた私案にもそれらが踏襲された(パヴロフが直接ホワイトと会ったのは、1941年5月の一度だけとのことである)。"
- These elements were incorporated into a private proposal written by White  
  _[ja]_ ← "具体的にはアメリカの圧力で満洲の関東軍を撤退させ、その見返りにアメリカが日本に経済的埋め合わせを行うというもので、ホワイトが書いた私案にもそれらが踏襲された(パヴロフが直接ホワイトと会ったのは、1941年5月の一度だけとのことである)。"
- Vitaly Pavlov met with White directly only once, in May 1941  
  _[ja]_ ← "具体的にはアメリカの圧力で満洲の関東軍を撤退させ、その見返りにアメリカが日本に経済的埋め合わせを行うというもので、ホワイトが書いた私案にもそれらが踏襲された(パヴロフが直接ホワイトと会ったのは、1941年5月の一度だけとのことである)。"
- White's private proposal was submitted to Henry Morgenthau, the Secretary of the Treasury, on June 6  
  _[ja]_ ← "しかし、ホワイトの私案は6月6日にヘンリー・モーゲンソー財務長官に提出されたものの、この時はモーゲンソーの興味を引かず「雪作戦」は空振りとなった。"
- Morgenthau was not interested in the proposal at that time  
  _[ja]_ ← "しかし、ホワイトの私案は6月6日にヘンリー・モーゲンソー財務長官に提出されたものの、この時はモーゲンソーの興味を引かず「雪作戦」は空振りとなった。"
- Operation Snow failed to gain traction  
  _[ja]_ ← "しかし、ホワイトの私案は6月6日にヘンリー・モーゲンソー財務長官に提出されたものの、この時はモーゲンソーの興味を引かず「雪作戦」は空振りとなった。"
- The Soviet Union's crisis intensified over the five months between June and November  
  _[ja]_ ← "しかもモーゲンソーが11月に私案を取り上げるまでの5ヶ月の間に、独ソ戦の開始・日本の関東軍特種演習の実施などソ連の危機は高まっており、そもそも作戦の成果には疑問符がつく。"
- The German-Soviet War began during this period  
  _[ja]_ ← "しかもモーゲンソーが11月に私案を取り上げるまでの5ヶ月の間に、独ソ戦の開始・日本の関東軍特種演習の実施などソ連の危機は高まっており、そもそも作戦の成果には疑問符がつく。"
- Japan's Kwantung Army conducted special exercises during this period  
  _[ja]_ ← "しかもモーゲンソーが11月に私案を取り上げるまでの5ヶ月の間に、独ソ戦の開始・日本の関東軍特種演習の実施などソ連の危機は高まっており、そもそも作戦の成果には疑問符がつく。"
- There are doubts about the effectiveness of the operation  
  _[ja]_ ← "しかもモーゲンソーが11月に私案を取り上げるまでの5ヶ月の間に、独ソ戦の開始・日本の関東軍特種演習の実施などソ連の危機は高まっており、そもそも作戦の成果には疑問符がつく。"
- Pavlov's testimony states that their thinking aligned with White's understanding of the situation  
  _[ja]_ ← "なお、パヴロフの証言によれば、自分達の考え方がホワイトの状況の理解と合致したと述べるに留まっており、ソ連の工作によりホワイトがモーゲンソー私案を書いたかは確定的ではない。"
- Pavlov's testimony only stated this alignment and did not go further  
  _[ja]_ ← "なお、パヴロフの証言によれば、自分達の考え方がホワイトの状況の理解と合致したと述べるに留まっており、ソ連の工作によりホワイトがモーゲンソー私案を書いたかは確定的ではない。"
- It is not certain whether White wrote the Morgenthau Private Plan due to Soviet operations  
  _[ja]_ ← "なお、パヴロフの証言によれば、自分達の考え方がホワイトの状況の理解と合致したと述べるに留まっており、ソ連の工作によりホワイトがモーゲンソー私案を書いたかは確定的ではない。"
- Pavlov completely rejected the idea that Japan and the United States would go to war  
  _[ja]_ ← "また、パヴロフが日米を開戦させるという考えを全面的に否定していること、モーゲンソー私案の内容が日本に融和的と言えることを踏まえると、ソ連が日米開戦を策したという「ソ連陰謀説」は成立しない。"
- The Morgenthau private plan could be considered conciliatory towards Japan  
  _[ja]_ ← "また、パヴロフが日米を開戦させるという考えを全面的に否定していること、モーゲンソー私案の内容が日本に融和的と言えることを踏まえると、ソ連が日米開戦を策したという「ソ連陰謀説」は成立しない。"
- Pavlov's testimony exaggerates White's influence  
  _[ja]_ ← "むしろパヴロフの証言は、ホワイトの影響力を誇張して、KGBの工作が日本を南進させてソ連を二正面作戦の危機から救ったというストーリーになっており、KGB称賛の意味合いが強い。"
- The narrative suggests KGB operations pushed Japan southward

### Other diplomatic and military factors  *(new section)*
- The document was also known as 'Schema di una base proposta per l'accordo fra gli Stati Uniti ed il Giappone'  
  _[it]_ ← "Outline of proposed Basis for Agreement Between The United States and Japan ("Schema di una base proposta per l'accordo fra gli Stati Uniti ed il Giappone")."
- Robert Craigie was the British Ambassador to Japan  
  _[ja]_ ← "ロバート・クレイギー駐日イギリス大使 「もし、暫定協定案について何らかの妥協が成立し、三ヵ月間の猶予期間が得られたとするならば、季節風の条件で日本軍のマレー上陸作戦は困難になっただろう。"
- Then the Japanese military's Malaya landing operation would have become difficult under monsoon conditions  
  _[ja]_ ← "ロバート・クレイギー駐日イギリス大使 「もし、暫定協定案について何らかの妥協が成立し、三ヵ月間の猶予期間が得られたとするならば、季節風の条件で日本軍のマレー上陸作戦は困難になっただろう。"
- The nature of the German-Soviet War would have changed  
  _[ja]_ ← "また独ソ戦の様相も変化する。"
- It would become extremely difficult for the Japanese government to reach a decision to go to war with the United States  
  _[ja]_ ← "対独潜水艦戦の成功といった新しい要素も加わり、日本政府が対米戦の決断に達することは極めて困難になるだろう」(1942年2月4日付チャーチル英首相宛の報告書において)。"
- New factors such as the success of anti-German submarine warfare were added  
  _[ja]_ ← "対独潜水艦戦の成功といった新しい要素も加わり、日本政府が対米戦の決断に達することは極めて困難になるだろう」(1942年2月4日付チャーチル英首相宛の報告書において)。"
- Wang Jingwei stated that he would step down at any time if his existence hindered a general peace  
  _[ja]_ ← "汪兆銘 ハル・ノートで汪兆銘政権は否認されることになるが、汪自身は「もし自分の存在が全面和平に妨害となるならば、何時にても引き下がる」と述べていた。"
- Wang was optimistic about negotiations between Japan and the United States  
  _[ja]_ ← "汪は日米交渉を楽観視していたが、畑俊六支那派遣軍総司令官から日米開戦を聞かされときは「頗る沈重なる面持」であったという。"
- Wang learned of the impending war between Japan and the United States from General Hata Junroku, commander of the China Expeditionary Army  
  _[ja]_ ← "汪は日米交渉を楽観視していたが、畑俊六支那派遣軍総司令官から日米開戦を聞かされときは「頗る沈重なる面持」であったという。"
- Upon hearing this news, Wang appeared very somber  
  _[ja]_ ← "汪は日米交渉を楽観視していたが、畑俊六支那派遣軍総司令官から日米開戦を聞かされときは「頗る沈重なる面持」であったという。"

## Knowledge conflicts (CONTRADICTED — human adjudication queue)

- **Non-English claim** _[ar]_: The agreement was reached at the Imperial Conference
  - English says: On November 5, 1941, Emperor Hirohito approved, in Imperial Conference, the plan for the attack on Pearl Harbor.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[de]_: Japan stationed troops in Indochina in July 1940
  - English says: In July 1941, Japanese military units occupied southern French Indochina, violating a gentlemen's agreement.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[es]_: On November 7, Ambassador Kichisaburō Nomura presented Plan A to Secretary of State Cordell Hull
  - English says: The first, Proposal A, was presented by him on November 6, 1941.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[es]_: The withdrawal of troops was listed as the fifth and final point in the original proposal
  - English says: The second section consists of 10 points and is titled 'Steps to be taken by the Government of the United States and by the Government of Japan'
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[es]_: Nomura and Kurusu delivered the complete Plan B personally
  - English says: On November 20, 1941, Nomura presented Proposal B
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[fa]_: Edward Miller described the action as the most destructive U.S. action against Japan
  - English says: This financial freeze was described by Miller as 'the most devastating American action against Japan'.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[fa]_: All military, naval, air, and police forces must withdraw from China and India
  - English says: withdraw of all military, naval, air and police forces from China and from Indo-China
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[fa]_: A commercial contract between the United States and Japan should be negotiated based on mutual behavior favorable to the people
  - English says: enter into negotiations for the conclusion between the United States and Japan of a trade agreement, based upon reciprocal most favored-nation treatment
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[fa]_: Toshio Tamogami was the chief of staff of the Japanese Air Force
  - English says: Toshio Tamogami, who was the Japan Air Self-Defense Force chief of staff, was forcibly retired by the Japanese government in 2008 for taking this position.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[fr]_: The Hull Note was officially titled 'Proposed Framework for an Agreement between the United States and Japan'
  - English says: The Hull note, officially the Outline of Proposed Basis for Agreement Between the United States and Japan
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[fr]_: Emperor Hirohito approved the war against the United States, the United Kingdom, and the Netherlands on December 8
  - English says: At an Imperial Conference on December 1, Emperor Hirohito approved attacks against the United States, the British Empire and the Dutch colonial empire.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ja]_: This decision was also made at the Imperial Conference on November 5
  - English says: On November 5, 1941, Emperor Hirohito approved, in Imperial Conference, the plan for the attack on Pearl Harbor.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ja]_: The Hal Note was not an official proposal from the US government.
  - English says: The Hull note, officially the Outline of Proposed Basis for Agreement Between the United States and Japan, was the final proposal delivered to the Empire of Japan by the United States of America
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ja]_: Japan was presented with only one principle
  - English says: The first section is a 'Draft mutual declaration of policy' by stating these principles: inviolability of territorial integrity... non-interference... equality... reliance upon international cooperation... non-discrimination... international economic cooperation... non-discriminatory access... full protection... establishment of such institutions...
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ja]_: The meeting involved Ambassador Yoshijirō Nomura of Japan to the United States
  - English says: Ambassador Kichisaburō Nomura presented two proposals to the American government.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ja]_: Harold I. 'Joe' Hull conveyed a rejection of the final Japanese proposal (Proposal B)
  - English says: The note, delivered on November 26, 1941, is named for Secretary of State Cordell Hull
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ja]_: The new conditions demanded an unconditional and complete withdrawal of Chinese forces from China
  - English says: withdraw of all military, naval, air and police forces from China and from Indo-China
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ja]_: The United States requested the denial of the Chinese government based in Chongqing
  - English says: no support (militarily, politically, economically) of any Government or regime in China other than the national Government of the Republic of China
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ko]_: In July 1941, the Japanese military abrogated the Shinko Agreement
  - English says: In July 1941, Japanese military units occupied southern French Indochina, violating a gentlemen's agreement.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ko]_: The Japanese government set the final deadline for negotiations with the United States as November 25
  - English says: They also believed that the Japanese foreign ministry had put an absolute deadline on negotiations of November 29 because 'after that things are automatically going to happen'.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ko]_: The Japanese government held a cabinet meeting on November 5, 1941
  - English says: On November 5, 1941, Emperor Hirohito approved, in Imperial Conference, the plan for the attack on Pearl Harbor.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ko]_: The US Army Intelligence Agency was decrypting Japanese diplomatic codes
  - English says: United States military intelligence had deciphered some of Japan's diplomatic codes so they knew that there was a second proposal in case it failed.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ko]_: The instruction was to present a counter-proposal
  - English says: He then decided that the Japanese were not being sincere in their negotiations and instructed Secretary Hull to drop the counterproposal.
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ru]_: The exclusion of Manchukuo was specified regarding the withdrawal from China
  - English says: withdraw of all military, naval, air and police forces from China and from Indo-China
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ru]_: Such international financial institutions and mechanisms were created
  - English says: establishment of such institutions and arrangements of international finances
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ru]_: The second section of the note was titled 'Measures that should be taken by the Government of the United States of America and the Government of Japan'
  - English says: The second section consists of 10 points and is titled "Steps to be taken by the Government of the United States and by the Government of Japan"
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[ru]_: A multilateral non-aggression pact was signed
  - English says: multilateral non-aggression pact among the British Empire, China, Japan, the Netherlands, the Soviet Union, Thailand and the United States
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z

- **Non-English claim** _[tr]_: The Hull Note was officially titled 'Draft of the Main Principles Offered Between the United States and Japan'
  - English says: The Hull note, officially the Outline of Proposed Basis for Agreement Between the United States and Japan, was the final proposal delivered to the Empire of Japan by the United States of America
  - Proposed reconciliation: 2023-12-28T22:21:33Z
